## [17.0.1.0.195] — 2026-07-23 — `107279dc`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+9/-4), project_task.py (+10/-1), stock_picking.py (+10/-1)

**Message:**
> fix(v195): scope field-lock xpath to skip embedded one2many views
>
> v192/v193/v194's readonly loops walked "//sheet//field" — which
> matches EVERY <field> element under the sheet, including those
> nested inside another <field>'s embedded one2many view (subtasks
> tab, timesheets, stock.moves, messages, followers, etc.).
> Those embedded fields belong to CHILD models (project.task's own
> messages one2many, stock.picking's move_ids_without_package
> pointing at stock.move, ...), which don't have
> helpdesk_ticket_id / sale_order_id / x_ticket_locked /
> x_studio_quotation_type / x_studio_helpdesk_ticket_id defined
> on them. When Odoo's ListRenderer evaluates the readonly
> expression per cell it hits
>     EvalError: Name 'helpdesk_ticket_id' is not defined
>     OwlError: An error occured in the owl lifecycle
> and the whole task-form modal crashes on render.
> Fix: add "[not(ancestor::field)]" to the xpath predicate on all
> three loops. That excludes any <field> nested inside another
> <field>'s sub-view, so the lock expression is stamped only on
> top-level task/ticket/picking fields where the referenced names
> resolve.
> Version 17.0.1.0.194 → 17.0.1.0.195.

**Stats:** 4 files changed, 30 insertions(+), 7 deletions(-)

---

> Third companion to v192 / v193 in the Fields-disable branch. Once
> a repair-flow picking transitions to state='done', every data
> field on its form should freeze — Odoo core respects state=done
> for most fields but Studio-added x_studio_* fields and some
> sub-tab widgets don't consistently honour it, leaving room for
> post-validation edits to drift the transfer record away from
> the physical movement it represents.
> Loop in stock_picking._get_view: walk every <field> inside
> <sheet>, OR its readonly with
>     (x_studio_quotation_type == 'Repair'
>        or x_studio_helpdesk_ticket_id)
>     and state == 'done'
> Gate covers both picking families:
>   * SO-driven delivery pickings — x_studio_quotation_type='Repair'
>     (mirrored from the SO by Studio automation at creation).
>   * Ticket-driven movement pickings (factory transit, dispatch,
>     return) — x_studio_helpdesk_ticket_id set, no quotation_type.
> Non-repair pickings untouched — Odoo's core state=done readonly
> still handles those.
> Same "invisible='1' means skip" guard as v192/v193 to avoid
> stamping readonly on pure marker fields, and same buttons-still-
> work property (Validate, Cancel, my workflow buttons aren't
> <field> elements).
> Version 17.0.1.0.193 → 17.0.1.0.194.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 39 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.193] — 2026-07-23 — `2ac13e66`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: project_task.py (+28/-0)

**Message:**
> feat(v193): lock task-form fields once linked SO exists
>
> Companion to v192 (ticket lock on first movement). Once a task's
> sale_order_id is populated — which happens the moment Plan
> Intervention confirms the SO for the linked ticket — the task
> form and every notebook page on it (Diagnosis, Repair Image,
> Warranty Card, Description, Extra Info, ...) freezes. Subsequent
> edits would drift the task's captured intake data away from
> whatever the SO / invoice / picking cycle has already booked
> against it.
> Adds a single xpath loop in project_task._get_view: walks every
> <field> inside <sheet>, ORs its readonly expression with
> "(helpdesk_ticket_id and sale_order_id)". Existing per-field
> readonly (Studio's own gates, product_id domain rules, user_id
> lock) stay intact; the SO-lock is layered on top.
> Scope stays repair-only via the helpdesk_ticket_id predicate —
> plain project.task records outside the repair chain
> (standalone tasks, non-FSM projects) retain full editability
> regardless of whether they happen to have a sale.order link.
> Buttons (Mark as Done, Products, smart buttons, Print gear)
> aren't <field> elements so the loop leaves them clickable —
> users can still progress the workflow, just can't rewrite the
> intake data once the commercial cycle has started.
> Version 17.0.1.0.192 → 17.0.1.0.193.

>      readonly rules (product_id domain, user_id lock, Studio's

> Factory / etc.), smart buttons (Tasks / Movements), the Print
> gear, and Back / × close actions all keep working — they're

## [17.0.1.0.191] — 2026-07-23 — `135d099e`

- **Model/field**: account_move.py (+18/-0)

**Message:**
> feat(v191): hide invoice Post until RUG account is set on RUG invoices
>
> RUG Approve flow needs the salesperson to reclassify the invoice
> lines to the company's RUG account BEFORE posting — the
> _rug_auto_settle side effect on action_post depends on those
> lines already sitting on the RUG account. Posting first,
> reclassifying second, leaves the balance stuck on Debtors
> without the offsetting RUG-account entry.
> Enforce the order by hiding the Post (Confirm) button while
> is_rug_invoice AND NOT is_rug_account_set. The existing "Change
> to RUG Account" button (v96-ish) is already visible in exactly
> that window and hides once the reclassification lands, so:
>   * Draft RUG invoice, lines on Debtors:
>       Change to RUG Account  ← visible
>       Post                   ← HIDDEN (v191)
>   * After clicking Change to RUG Account:
>       Change to RUG Account  ← hidden (is_rug_account_set=True)
>       Post                   ← visible
>   * Non-RUG (customer-pays / non-repair) invoices:
>       Both buttons behave exactly as before v191.
> Preserves whatever invisible expression Odoo core / other
> inherits already stamped on action_post via
> "(existing) or (is_rug_invoice and not is_rug_account_set)".

## [17.0.1.0.190] — 2026-07-23 — `6a1d1cdc`

**Message:**
> feat(v190): replace repair-data-missing buttons with sticky toasts
>
> Two Studio buttons sat on the task-form header:
>   * View Repair Diagnosis Validation (action 2224)
>   * View Repair Image Validation     (action 2242)
> Both were only visible when the corresponding data was missing —
> clicking them navigated to a separate action to fill it in. The
> buttons cluttered the form and forced a navigate-away step for
> what should be a quick data-entry nudge.
> Replace with sticky toast notifications. On every project.task
> form load, if the task is a repair task (helpdesk_ticket_id set)
> AND the diagnosis / image data is empty, dispatch a warning
> notification per missing field. Notifications are:
>   * Sticky — stay until the salesperson populates the data or
>     dismisses them manually.
>   * Auto-dismissed programmatically when the missing field flips
>     to a set value (on onRecordSaved re-check).
>   * Cleared on form unmount so switching to a different task
>     doesn't leave orphan notifications.

> a second toast for the same missing field on the same open form.

>   * Buttons removed from the sanitized task-form arch —
>     _sanitize_studio_task_form's clean_arch template drops both
>     button rows and bumps the marker to v190 so the sanitize
>     re-runs on upgrade.

>     patches FormController.prototype. Scoped to
>     resModel === 'project.task' so no-op cost on other forms.
>   * Manifest declares the new web.assets_backend bundle.
> Non-repair project.task records (helpdesk_ticket_id unset) never
> see the toasts.

## [17.0.1.0.189] — 2026-07-23 — `a02cc0d8`

**Changed:**

- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+30/-4)

**Message:**
> feat(v189): hide Re-estimate + Unlock buttons when SO has an invoice
>
> Once an invoice exists on a sale.order, both re-estimating and
> unlocking the SO would create a ledger / workflow mismatch: the
> invoice is a posted accounting document, and quietly resetting
> the SO to draft (or unlocking a locked one) leaves the ledger
> referencing a state that no longer exists on the source
> document. The salesperson should consciously cancel or reverse
> the invoice first, then progress the SO from there.
> Two hides, both gated on invoice_count > 0:
>   * Re-estimate button (Fix-repair inject in header):
>       invisible extended from "not can_re_estimate" to
>       "not can_re_estimate or invoice_count > 0"
>   * Unlock button (Odoo core action_unlock, visible when
>     state='done'):
>       existing invisible expression ANDed with
>       "invoice_count > 0" via
>       "(existing) or (invoice_count > 0)" so the standard
>       state != 'done' gate stays and the invoice guard adds
>       on top.
> Non-invoiced SOs continue to see both buttons at their standard
> visibility (Re-estimate when signed and no outgoing delivery
> done; Unlock when state='done').
> Version 17.0.1.0.188 → 17.0.1.0.189.

## [17.0.1.0.188] — 2026-07-23 — `d9c1b7a1`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_payment_register.py (+22/-40)

**Message:**
> fix(v188): tighten payment-amount preservation to freeze after first compute
>
> v187 tracked user "touched" state via onchange, but the intent is
> stricter than that: the salesperson wants the wizard to compute
> Amount ONCE (on initial open, to the invoice residual) and then
> NEVER touch it again — regardless of whether the change came
> from the user typing a partial amount or from a Journal switch.
> Drop the touched-flag machinery. Instead: preserve Amount on
> every recompute where wizard.amount is already non-zero. Wizard
> opens with amount=0, super() fills it with the residual on first
> compute, and every subsequent compute (Journal change, currency
> change, any depends invalidation) hits the preservation branch
> and keeps whatever the field already holds.
> Edge case: user deliberately sets Amount to 0 then changes
> Journal — the field snaps back to the residual. Not a real
> workflow (Rs. 0 payments are meaningless) so this is an
> acceptable trade for the simpler logic and no extra fields on
> the wizard.
> Version 17.0.1.0.187 → 17.0.1.0.188.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 23 insertions(+), 41 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.187] — 2026-07-23 — `97c71bde`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_payment_register.py (+53/-1)

**Message:**
> fix(v187): preserve typed Amount across Journal change on payment register
>
> On the Register Payment wizard, Odoo's _compute_amount depends
> on currency_id (which changes when the salesperson picks a
> different Journal). That recompute unconditionally rewrites the
> Amount field back to the invoice's full residual — a partial
> payment amount the user typed gets silently overwritten. Not
> just annoying — a massive oversight risk: a Rs. 30,000 advance
> becomes a Rs. 500,000 payment against the wrong journal.
> Trap the user's touch on Amount via a non-stored boolean
> x_amount_user_touched (onchange fires only on client-side edits,
> never on server-side recomputes) and set it True on the first
> manual write. Override _compute_amount to save touched rows'
> amount before super() runs, then restore after. Untouched rows
> still get Odoo's default behaviour (wizard opens with the
> invoice's residual auto-filled).
> Also flip the sentiment: "once touched, stays touched" — even if
> the user retypes the full residual by hand, we treat that as an
> explicit user choice and preserve it across Journal changes.
> Version 17.0.1.0.186 → 17.0.1.0.187.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 54 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.186] — 2026-07-23 — `8b125dd8`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_payment_register.py (+46/-4)

**Message:**
> fix(v186): advance ticket to "Advance Received" on 1st invoice payment
>
> The transition from Estimation Approval Received → Advance Received
> was already wired in account.payment.register.action_create_payments
> (v163-era) but two guards were too tight for the actual workflow:
>   1. Accepted payment_states = ('in_payment', 'paid'). First
>      payment on a repair invoice is typically a partial advance
>      (e.g. 50% of the SO total), so the invoice sits at 'partial'
>      after that first post. The 'partial' state was excluded, so
>      the ticket move never fired for the exact case the workflow
>      was designed to catch. Add 'partial' to the acceptance list.
>   2. SO filter = customer_pays or rug_rejected. Broadened to
>      "any Repair SO whose RUG cycle isn't approved yet" — covers
>      the two original cases plus the edge where a warranty repair
>      has an invoice created manually before RUG approval landed.
>   3. Ticket-stage gate = exact match on 'Estimation Approval
>      Received'. If the ticket happened to be at an earlier stage
>      (e.g. late-arriving payment before the salesperson advanced
>      the stage), the move was skipped. Widen the gate to any
>      pre-Advance-Received stage: New, Sent to Factory, Received
>      at Factory, Diagnosis, Estimation Sent to Customer, and
>      Estimation Approval Received. Never regress a ticket already
>      at or past Advance Received.
> Two class-level frozensets host the widened stage list and the
> accepted payment-state tuple so the intent is scan-readable and
> future edits only touch one place.
> Version 17.0.1.0.185 → 17.0.1.0.186.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 47 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.185] — 2026-07-23 — `d5871186`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **View**: helpdesk_ticket_views.xml (+17/-3)

**Message:**
> fix(v185): use readonly=1 instead of clickable=0 to disable stage nav
>
> v183/v184 tried options="{'clickable': '0'}" but Odoo evaluates
> that value through exprToBoolean, and the STRING '0' is truthy
> (any non-empty string is truthy in Python — bool('0') == True).
> The widget saw clickable=True and stage pills stayed clickable.
> Switch to readonly="1" — the statusbar widget respects the
> field's readonly state and suppresses its click handlers when
> readonly is set. Works universally in Odoo 17 and doesn't depend
> on the exact truthiness of the clickable option.
> Workflow buttons in the ticket header (Send to Factory / Received
> at Factory / Send to Sales Centre / Received at Sales Centre)
> advance the ticket via _move_to_stage() which sudo-writes to
> stage_id, bypassing the view-level readonly. Stage progression
> through the intended workflow path remains fully functional.
> Version 17.0.1.0.184 → 17.0.1.0.185.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 18 insertions(+), 4 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.184] — 2026-07-23 — `da06d055`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **View**: helpdesk_ticket_views.xml (+11/-1)

**Message:**
> fix(v184): relax stage_id xpath so v183 view inherit installs
>
> v183 failed to install with
>   Element '<xpath expr="//field[@name='stage_id'][@widget='statusbar']">'
>   cannot be located in parent view
> Odoo validates view inheritance against the DIRECT PARENT view
> at install time (helpdesk.helpdesk_ticket_view_form here, id
> 3972). The base view declares
>     <field name="stage_id"/>
> without the widget attribute — widget="statusbar" is added by a
> downstream inherit (helpdesk_stage or similar). Combined
> attribute matching in the xpath therefore never resolves and
> Odoo rejects the module upgrade.
> Drop the widget=statusbar predicate. The remaining
>     //field[@name='stage_id']
> is unique in the form (only one stage_id declaration) so the
> attribute add lands on the right element, and the clickable
> option merges into the fully-resolved arch alongside the
> downstream-added widget so runtime behaviour is unchanged from
> what v183 intended.
> Version 17.0.1.0.183 → 17.0.1.0.184.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 12 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.172] — 2026-07-21 — `2cb79bb2`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-2)
- **Model/field**: project_task.py (+18/-0)
- **View**: -project_task_views.xml (+0/-38)

**Message:**
> fix: hide task fields via _get_view (base parent doesn't contain them all)
>
> v170's project_task_views.xml inherited from project.view_task_form2
> (the base task form) and xpath'd four fields:
>   tag_ids
>   sale_line_id
>   planned_date_begin
>   x_studio_material_availability
> Upgrade failed at load-time with
>   Element '<xpath expr="//field[@name='sale_line_id']">' cannot be
>   located in parent view
> Only tag_ids lives in the base view (project.view_task_form2). The
> other three are inserted by sale_project, project_enterprise, and
> studio_customization respectively — my inherit's xpath validation
> runs against the direct parent, not the fully-merged arch, so those
> fields aren't present when Odoo checks and the record refuses to
> install.
> Fix: delete views/project_task_views.xml and its manifest entry.
> Move all four hides into the existing project.task._get_view
> override — that operates on the fully-merged arch produced by
> super()._get_view(), where every module's inherits are already
> applied. `xpath("//field[@name='X']")` gracefully returns [] when
> a field isn't present on a particular install.
> Same UX outcome — fields hidden, DB columns / computes / constraints
> untouched — with a load path that doesn't fail on install order.
> Version 17.0.1.0.171 → 17.0.1.0.172.

## [17.0.1.0.167] — 2026-07-15 — `7e57b2f6`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: stock_picking.py (+59/-21)

**Message:**
> feat(non-rug): block delivery Validate until an invoice payment lands
>
> Previously nuw_block_validate unblocked the delivery Validate
> button as soon as ANY invoice existed on the SO — including a
> fresh draft one that no money had touched. Combined with the
> v163 single-full-invoice flow (which creates the invoice in
> draft immediately after confirm), operators could validate a
> delivery before the customer paid anything.
> Tighten the gate: unblock only when at least one non-cancelled
> invoice on the SO has payment_state in the customer-has-paid
> set — ('partial', 'in_payment', 'paid'). Anything else
> ('not_paid', 'reversed', 'invoicing_legacy') keeps the block.
> Scope
> -----
>   * Customer-pays repair SOs only (x_repair_customer_pays or
>     x_studio_rug_rejected). Warranty-approved and non-Repair
>     SOs stay untouched — nuw_block_validate remains False for
>     them.
> Belt and braces
> ---------------
>   * @api.depends now watches invoice_ids.state AND
>     invoice_ids.payment_state so the field recomputes on every
>     invoice state change or payment registration event.
>   * button_validate() overrides super() and re-checks
>     nuw_block_validate server-side, raising the same reason as
>     the (now-tightened) view hide message. Prevents the gate
>     being circumvented via a URL / API call directly hitting
>     button_validate.
> Dropped the previous stage-name fallback ("Advance Received" /
> "Repair Started") — that path predated the invoice.payment_state
> signal and was really trying to approximate the same thing.
> Reading payment_state directly is simpler and more accurate.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 60 insertions(+), 22 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.166] — 2026-07-15 — `4811145b`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_payment_register.py (+85/-0)

**Message:**
> feat(non-rug): enforce advance-payment threshold on invoice payments
>
> Users can now register partial payments against the single full
> invoice created by v163's non-RUG flow. This commit adds a hard
> guard on account.payment.register.action_create_payments so a
> first payment below the company's configured advance-payment
> percentage cannot be posted.
> Rule
> ----
> For every invoice on the wizard whose linked sale.order has
> x_repair_customer_pays=True or x_studio_rug_rejected=True:
>   cumulative_paid_after_this_payment
>       = (invoice.amount_total - invoice.amount_residual)
>       + wizard.amount
>   threshold_amount
>       = SO.amount_total * (x_minimum_sales_margin.x_studio_advance_payment_ / 100)
>       # x_minimum_sales_margin row scoped to SO.company_id
>   if cumulative_paid_after_this_payment < threshold_amount:
>       raise UserError("Advance Payment should be at least ...")
> Once cumulative paid crosses the threshold, subsequent payments
> carry no minimum — the second payment can be any amount because
> the projected cumulative is already >= threshold at that point.
> Scope
> -----
>   * Non-RUG (customer-pays / Reject-RUG) repair SOs only.
>     RUG-approved and non-Repair SOs pass through untouched.
>   * out_invoice moves only. Refunds (out_refund) are ignored —
>     a refund is not a customer payment.
>   * Config lookup is per-company: reads the x_minimum_sales_margin
>     row for SO.company_id. Missing row or 0% threshold = no gate
>     (skip validation).
> Also
> ----
> The Studio server action 2427 (RR - Validate Payment %) is still
> active as base_automation with trigger='on_change'. Its guard
> checks record.x_studio_quotation_type == 'Repair' which is never
> set on wizard-created payments (checked against the last five
> posted payments — all have x_studio_quotation_type=false), so
> it's a dormant no-op in the current flow. Not touched here.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 86 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.165] — 2026-07-15 — `4533b362`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+40/-6)

**Message:**
> fix(dispatch): gate on invoice.payment_state, not amount_unpaid
>
> The Dispatch button's payment gate relied on:
>   so_fully_paid = invoice_status == 'invoiced' AND amount_unpaid == 0
> That combination gave false positives after the v163 single-full-
> invoice flow: _create_repair_full_invoice sets qty_invoiced =
> product_uom_qty on every order line, so invoice_status flips to
> 'invoiced' the instant the draft invoice is written. Meanwhile
> Odoo's amount_unpaid compute only sums residuals on POSTED
> invoices — a fresh draft is excluded entirely, so amount_unpaid
> = 0. Both conditions True → so_fully_paid True → Dispatch shows
> before the customer has paid anything.
> Rewrite the gate to inspect invoice payment state directly:
>   for each linked task SO:
>     - must have at least one non-cancelled invoice
>     - every non-cancelled invoice must be state='posted'
>       AND payment_state in ('in_payment', 'paid')
> 'in_payment' counts alongside 'paid' — the customer has already
> handed over the money; bank reconciliation is a downstream
> accounting step that shouldn't block operations. The trigger
> still runs when either state or payment_state on the linked
> account.move records changes.
> is_tested_ok and is_so_cancelled bypasses on the Dispatch view
> expression remain untouched — those flows never invoice at all
> so the payment gate doesn't apply.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 41 insertions(+), 7 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.164] — 2026-07-15 — `5efd0677`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+13/-2)

**Message:**
> fix(non-rug): hide Create Invoice reliably once an invoice exists — use invoice_count
>
> v163's Create Invoice invisible expression relied on
> invoice_status == 'invoiced' as the "already invoiced" signal.
> That check misses a real case: SOs from the legacy two-invoice
> wizard flow (down-payment invoice + final invoice) end up with
> a lingering down-payment product line whose qty_invoiced can be
> zero and a real product line whose qty_invoiced is also zero
> (when the down-payment invoice landed on the down-payment
> service line, not the real line). Result: invoice_status stays
> 'no' even with a live down-payment invoice on the SO — button
> shows despite an active invoice being present.
> Fix: switch to invoice_count > 0. Odoo's invoice_count on
> sale.order is computed after filtering out state='cancel'
> moves, so:
>   - Any non-cancelled invoice (posted OR draft) hides the
>     button, regardless of what qty_invoiced looks like on the
>     order lines.
>   - When every invoice on the SO gets cancelled, invoice_count
>     returns to 0 and the button reappears — the exact
>     "cancelled invoice, please re-invoice" ask.
> No change to the routing / method behaviour — same
> action_repair_create_invoice, same _create_repair_full_invoice.
> Only the visibility rule tightens.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 14 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.163] — 2026-07-15 — `f4f488a6`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+158/-24)

**Message:**
> feat(non-rug): single full invoice on Create Invoice — no more down-payment split
>
> Before v163, non-RUG (customer-pays or Reject-RUG) repair sale
> orders went through Odoo's standard two-invoice down-payment
> flow:
>   1. Percentage 'Create Invoice' button opens the
>      sale.advance.payment.inv wizard → creates a Down Payment
>      invoice for N % of the SO total.
>   2. Delivery is validated → invoice_status flips to 'to invoice'.
>   3. Purple 'Create Invoice' → creates the final invoice for the
>      remaining amount, with a negative down-payment adjustment
>      line so the two invoices sum to the SO total.
> For quick repairs where the customer pays 100 % upfront the
> two-step split is friction. The purple button was also hidden
> until delivery (invoice_status != 'to invoice' — pure Odoo
> built-in behaviour driven by invoice_policy='delivery' on the
> repair products), forcing every non-RUG SO through the down-
> payment path even when the customer was ready to pay in full.
> v163 changes:
>   * Purple 'Create Invoice' repointed at a new router method
>     action_repair_create_invoice on sale.order (type='object',
>     no wizard).
>       - Non-RUG SOs  → _create_repair_full_invoice: builds ONE
>         account.move for the entire SO using product_uom_qty on
>         every real line (ignoring qty_to_invoice / delivery
>         gating). Section / note lines preserved; is_downpayment
>         legacy lines skipped.
>       - RUG-approved SOs → standard _create_invoices() (delivered-
>         qty invoice, no down-payment wizard).
>       - Non-Repair SOs → standard _create_invoices() unchanged.
>   * Purple button invisible expression rewritten so it becomes
>     visible on non-RUG SOs immediately after confirm — no
>     delivery gate. RUG-approved keeps the standard
>     'invoice_status in (to_invoice, upselling)' gate.
>   * Percentage 'Create Invoice' hidden on every Repair SO — the
>     down-payment path is no longer used for repairs. Non-Repair
>     SOs (Sales / Project) keep the button.
> Guards on the new method: state must be 'sale', no existing non-
> cancelled invoices, at least one non-downpayment positive-qty
> line to invoice. Raises UserError with a clear message otherwise.
> qty_invoiced on sale.order.line updates automatically via
> Odoo's standard sale_line_ids link — _prepare_invoice_line
> already sets it.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 159 insertions(+), 25 deletions(-)

> Python declarations first, then flip state.

**Message:**

>

> leftover repair fields, but the write silently rolled back for
> every one of them — post-upgrade audit still showed all 11 as
> state='manual'. Same class of block we hit on ir_model.state
> during the v141 catalogue migration: some field configurations
> (One2many with a still-manual comodel like x_task_diagnosis,

> x_studio_incomplete_delivery_available, etc.) trigger an

> transition.

> so the registry sees the new value. Data columns untouched.

> now all show module='Fix-repair' with the expected action_<id>

## [17.0.1.0.158] — 2026-07-14 — `64c0cc54`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+21/-2)

**Message:**
> v158: fix v157 SQL — arch_db is jsonb, not text
>
> v157's raw SQL passed a bare XML string to a jsonb column and
> psycopg2 rejected it with:
>   psycopg2.errors.InvalidTextRepresentation:
>       invalid input syntax for type json
>   LINE 1: UPDATE ir_ui_view SET arch_db = '<data>
>   DETAIL: Token "<" is invalid.
> In Odoo 17 ir_ui_view.arch_db is stored as jsonb with per-language
> keys ({'en_US': '<data>...'}, and optionally other langs). Wrap
> the clean arch in that json shape, merging into the existing lang
> dict so any non-default translation survives, and cast to jsonb
> in the UPDATE. json module imported at the top of
> helpdesk_ticket.py.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 22 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)

- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+130/-65)

**Message:**
> v157: rewrite view 3019 arch with robust name-based xpaths via SQL
>
> Iterating one broken xpath at a time turned into a losing game:
> v155 caught action_fsm_create_quotation, v156 caught
> action_fsm_view_material, upgrade then surfaced the positional
> sale_order_id xpath, and more positional ones were queued
> behind it.
> Rewrite view 3019's arch_db in one pass with stable name-based
> xpaths, dropping the two industry_fsm sibling-chain button
> attribute changes (both already replicated verbatim by
> project_task.py._get_view).

>   - 3 repair-workflow buttons after //field[@name=
>     'personal_stage_type_id']
>   - user_ids → 'Assignees' rename + helpdesk_ticket_id /
>     x_studio_created_date / x_studio_repair_reason fields after

>     x_studio_material_availability trio after //field[@name=
>     'sale_order_id']
>   - The 3 Studio notebook pages (Repair Image, Warranty Card,
>     Repair Diagnosis with the diagnosis line editor) inside
>     //notebook
> Raw SQL bypasses ir.ui.view.write()'s _check_xml validator,
> which combines the *live* combined arch during the upgrade
> transaction and can raise on unrelated in-flight state. After
> the SQL update + arch cache invalidation the next form combine
> sees only clean name-based xpaths.
> Idempotent via a marker comment on the second line of arch_db —
> subsequent runs skip the rewrite.

> So both Studio xpaths are redundant. v156 strips them both in
> one pass, and writes with context no_check_xml=True to bypass
> arch re-validation during the transaction (validation already
> ran when Studio saved the view years ago; a pure content strip
> introduces no new inheritance specs).
> Idempotent — the loop no-ops on any button whose xpath is
> already gone.

- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+109/-0)

**Message:**
> v154: hotfix — realign QWeb template `key` field with the new module prefix
>
> v153 renamed ir.actions.report.report_name from
> 'studio_customization.<tail>' to 'Fix-repair.<tail>' but left the
> underlying QWeb template's ir.ui.view.key column unchanged. The
> website module's ir_ui_view._get_view_id() resolves report
> templates by matching key verbatim and does not fall back to
> ir.model.data — so after v153 every report on the repair scope
> 500'd with:
>   ValueError: View 'Fix-repair.studio_report_docume_<uuid>' in
>   website 1 not found
> _fix_studio_report_template_keys walks every Fix-repair-owned
> report, resolves its template by the still-Studio key, and:
>   1. rewrites the key from 'studio_customization.<tail>' to
>      'Fix-repair.<tail>';
>   2. creates the ir.model.data pin under Fix-repair.<tail> if
>      one didn't exist (Studio never gave the copy-of-copy
>      templates their own pins — they existed only as ir.ui.view
>      rows resolvable via key);
>   3. rewrites any t-call="studio_customization.<migrated_tail>"
>      occurrences inside arch_db so chained inheritance resolves
>      under the new prefix.
> Idempotent. Bounded to tails already in scope so unrelated
> Studio templates outside the repair migration are untouched.

## [17.0.1.0.145] — 2026-07-13 — `98c53264`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+164/-6)

**Message:**
> refactor(helpdesk): migrate 6 button-triggered server actions to Python (Tier 2)
>
> Ports the six button-triggered Studio server actions for the core
> repair workflow (Send/Receive Factory/Centre + Cancel/Reopen) to
> native Python methods. Same one-line delegation pattern as Tier 1
> (v144).
> Server actions rewired:
>   2001 'RR - Send to Factory'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_send_to_factory
>     Sets repair_location + send_to_factory + audit + stage
>     (5/24 per company).
>   2002 'RR - Receive at Factory'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_receive_at_factory
>     Sets receive_at_factory + audit + stage (6/25).
>   2007 'RR - Send to Sales Centre'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_send_to_sales_centre
>     Sets send_to_centre + audit + stage (7/26).
>   2006 'RR - Receive at Sales Centre'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_receive_at_sales_centre
>     Sets receive_at_centre + audit + stage (8/27).
>   2220 'RR - Cancel Repair'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_cancel_repair
>     Guards on cancel_reason, records cancelled_stage_id,
>     flips cancelled=True, records cancelled_by/date/status,
>     stage (4/23).
>   2221 'RR - Reopen Repair'
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_studio_reopen_repair
>     Restores original stage, flips cancelled=False /
>     reopened=True, records reopened_by/date/status.
> Method-naming distinction: these _repair_studio_* methods
> mirror ONLY what the Studio server actions did (flag + stage +
> audit). Fix-repair already has separate `action_*` methods on
> the same class (action_send_to_factory, action_send_to_sales_centre,
> etc.) that do MORE (create pickings). Those are called from
> Fix-repair's own buttons via view inheritance and are unaffected.
> Keeping the two paths distinct preserves original behaviour
> regardless of which button the user clicks.
> Migration extends the existing
> _delegate_studio_server_actions_to_native so the same idempotent
> hook flips these six as well. Guard substrings chosen to be
> unique to each action (e.g. 'x_studio_created_by_10' for the
> receive-at-centre action).
> Remaining server actions: ~19 (heavy compute Repair Route +
> Serial Nos, RUG duplicates, email actions, misc).

> delegation pattern Fix-repair already uses for compute delegations
> (see sale_order._delegate_studio_computes_to_native).

> delegation into a native Python method):
>   id 1976 'RR - Repair Seq.No'                  (on_create_or_write)
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_seq_no_on_create_or_write

>   id 2000 'RR - Auto Populate Repair Location'  (on_change)
>     → helpdesk.ticket._repair_populate_repair_location

>     On serial-number set, walks outgoing move-line → source SO

> already carry it. Also skips when the guard substring is absent
> (someone manually edited the code — don't overwrite).

> templates) coming in follow-up commits.

## [17.0.1.0.143] — 2026-07-13 — `0b37fd47`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: repair_master_data.py (+40/-0)

**Message:**
> fix(migration): create Fix-repair ir.model.data pins for repair models
>
> v142 upgrade completed, ir.model.state flipped to 'base', but
> runtime RPC calls to x_repair_accounts et al. return
>   KeyError: 'x_repair_accounts'
> because the ir.model rows have modules='' — no owning module.
> Without an ir.model.data pin linking the ir.model row to
> Fix-repair, Odoo's registry-init can't associate the row with
> our Python classes, so the classes never register into
> pool.models.
> The AbstractModel x_repair_master_data.migration works because
> Odoo auto-created a pin for it (it's a brand-new class name).
> The five catalogue models pre-existed as Studio-manual, so Odoo
> saw the ir.model row already there and skipped the auto-pin
> step — leaving them orphaned after we unlinked the Studio pins.
> Fix: after unlinking studio_customization pins, CREATE
> Fix-repair ir.model.data pins for both the model rows AND their
> field rows. Naming matches Odoo's auto-generated pattern
> (model_x_repair_accounts, field_x_repair_accounts__x_name, etc).
> Idempotent: skips creation when a Fix-repair pin already exists.
> After this fix, v143 upgrade will:
>   1. Model states already base (no-op, safe)
>   2. Field states already base (no-op)
>   3. Studio pins already gone (no-op)
>   4. Create the missing Fix-repair pins → registry-next-load
>      will register the Python classes into pool.models → RPC
>      calls resolve correctly.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 41 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.142] — 2026-07-13 — `c6a12c08`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: repair_master_data.py (+17/-4)

**Message:**
> fix(migration): use raw SQL for ir.model.fields.state on new models
>
> v141 upgrade rolled back with:
>   KeyError: 'x_repair_reason_custom'
>   File ".../addons/base/models/ir_model.py", line 1101, in write
>     models = self.pool.descendants(patched_models, '_inherits')
>   File ".../modules/registry.py", line 222, in descendants
>     model = self[queue.popleft()]
> ir.model.fields.write({'state': 'base'}) internally walks
> pool.descendants(patched_models, '_inherits'), which tries to
> look up each patched model in registry.pool.models. Our freshly-
> added Python-defined x_repair_* classes are NOT yet in
> pool.models at data-XML-load time — they get registered later
> in the module load cycle. Result: KeyError.
> Previous cluster migrations were unaffected because their fields
> belong to well-known models (helpdesk.ticket, project.task,
> res.users) that are always in the registry.
> Fix: same raw-SQL workaround as for ir.model in v141 — direct
> UPDATE bypasses the descendants traversal + registry lookup.
> Safe: we only touch the state column on rows we own, and the
> Python fields are already registered at runtime.
> After this fix, v142 upgrade should complete cleanly with all
> five model rows AND their field rows flipped to state='base'.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 18 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.141] — 2026-07-13 — `795f4d13`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: repair_master_data.py (+18/-5)

**Message:**
> fix(migration): use raw SQL for ir.model.state flip
>
> v140 upgrade rolled back with:
>   UserError: Field "Type" cannot be modified on models.
>   File ".../addons/base/models/ir_model.py", line 365, in write
> Odoo's ir.model.write() has a hard UserError guard on the 'state'
> column — you can't flip a model row's state through the ORM. The
> guard is not permission-based (sudo doesn't help); it's a
> validation-level block.
> Switch the ir.model state flip to a raw
>   UPDATE ir_model SET state = 'base' WHERE id IN (...)
> Safe here because:
>   - We only touch the state column on rows we own
>   - The Python classes are already registered at this point, so
>     the flip just aligns metadata with the actual runtime state
>   - No dependent invariants are broken (no cascade, no FK check)
> ir.model.fields.state writes stay on the ORM path — that write
> IS permitted and every previous cluster migration proved it.
> After this fix, v141 upgrade should complete cleanly with all
> five model rows AND their field rows flipped to state='base'.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 19 insertions(+), 6 deletions(-)

---

**Message:**
> refactor(helpdesk): migrate 5 custom Studio catalogue models to Python
>
> Ports the five Studio-manual custom catalogue models to native
> Python-defined models. Same names, same fields (verbatim from
> Studio's ir.model.fields rows), same mail.thread inheritance.
> DB tables and existing rows preserved.
> Models moved:
>   x_repair_accounts        2 rows   Per-company RUG account map
>   x_repair_reason          4 rows   Internal repair reason list
>   x_repair_reason_custom  12 rows   Customer-facing reason list
>   x_repair_stages          7 rows   Standalone stage catalogue
>   x_repair_sub_reason      4 rows   Sub-reasons under a reason
> Each class defines the 5 custom fields per Studio's schema:
>   x_active                Boolean
>   x_name                  Char (display name — _rec_name)
>   x_studio_company_id     Many2one res.company
>   x_studio_sequence       Integer
>   + one specialty field per model (x_studio_rug_account,
>     x_color, x_studio_description, or x_studio_reason_code)
> Inherits mail.thread + mail.activity.mixin per Studio's default
> model shape, so the ~24 activity/mail helper fields (message_ids,
> activity_ids, etc.) auto-attach without explicit declaration.
> Migration is different from the field-only clusters — needs to
> flip TWO ir.model layers:
>   1. ir.model row state 'manual' → 'base' (5 rows)
>   2. All ir.model.fields rows for those models to 'base'
>   3. Drop studio_customization pins on both layers
> Wrapped in a new AbstractModel `x_repair_master_data.migration`
> so the migration function can be called via the data XML
> <function> hook without needing to live on a Studio-owned model.
> New file: models/repair_master_data.py (all 5 model classes +
> migration abstract model).
> Wired into models/__init__.py + data/fix_repair_data.xml.
> Studio-generated views (form/tree/kanban) and act_window actions
> for these models remain in ir.ui.view / ir.actions.act_window as
> studio_customization records — they resolve fields by name, so
> they continue to work against the Python-defined models.

## [17.0.1.0.130] — 2026-07-13 — `33aaf033`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+50/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+6/-0)

**Message:**
> refactor(helpdesk): complete RUG cluster migration — remove Studio ownership
>
> v129 defined the seven RUG cycle fields in Python but the
> ir.model.fields DB rows still showed state='manual' and were
> pinned to the studio_customization module via ir.model.data.
> That meant Studio's UI still treated them as editable Studio
> fields, contrary to the "just in the python model, not in
> Studio" migration goal.
> _migrate_studio_rug_cluster_to_base on helpdesk.ticket:
>   1. Flips state 'manual' → 'base' on the seven cluster rows
>      — Studio no longer manages the field definitions.
>   2. Unlinks the studio_customization ir.model.data pins on
>      those rows — uninstalling studio_customization can't drop
>      them, and Fix-repair effectively owns the field metadata.
> Called from data/fix_repair_data.xml <function> hook on every
> install/upgrade. Idempotent — subsequent runs find no manual
> rows to flip and no studio_customization pins to unlink.
> Does NOT delete any ir.model.fields row and does NOT drop any
> DB column. All existing field values are preserved.
> Verify after upgrade with:
>   env['ir.model.fields'].search_read(
>       [('model','=','helpdesk.ticket'),
>        ('name','like','x_studio_rug_')],
>       ['name','state','modules'])
> Expected: state='base' on all seven RUG rows, and 'modules'
> no longer contains studio_customization.

**Stats:** 3 files changed, 57 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.128] — 2026-07-09 — `da95fc77`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+13/-9)

**Message:**
> fix(sale): tax_totals_json alias must return JSON string, not dict
>
> v127 exposed tax_totals_json as fields.Json returning a dict.
> Portal-accept PDF render then failed one layer deeper with:
>   TypeError: the JSON object must be str, bytes or bytearray,
>   not dict
>   File "/home/odoo/src/odoo/odoo/tools/json.py", line 26, in loads
> The Studio-migrated arch does `json.loads(record.tax_totals_json)`
> inside the template because in Odoo 16 that field was a Char
> storing the JSON *string*. Odoo 17 renamed it to `tax_totals` and
> made it a native dict (fields.Json). Passing our dict-returning
> alias into json.loads blows up.
> Fix: switch the alias to fields.Char and json.dumps() the value.
> The template then round-trips through json.loads() back to a
> dict and renders as expected.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 14 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.127] — 2026-07-09 — `9b929e4f`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+18/-0)

**Message:**
> fix(sale): expose tax_totals_json alias for legacy Studio arch
>
> Portal quote-accept PDF render on the v17-final env fails with:
>   AttributeError: 'sale.order' object has no attribute 'tax_totals_json'
>   Template: sale.report_saleorder_document
>   Node: <t t-call="account.document_tax_totals"/>
> Pre-existing issue exposed once v126's report-anchor fix let the
> template past the t-else compile error. The Studio-modified arch
> on this instance was migrated from Odoo 16 and still calls
> `account.document_tax_totals` expecting `record.tax_totals_json`,
> but Odoo 17 replaced that attribute with `tax_totals`.
> Rather than hand-editing every Studio-owned QWeb template to
> rename the reference, expose tax_totals under the old field name
> too. Both point at the same in-memory dict — the alias is a
> computed Json field with a `@api.depends('tax_totals')` so the
> ORM invalidates it whenever the underlying value changes.
> Not a great long-term solution (real fix is to update the Studio
> template to use `tax_totals`), but unblocks portal signing today
> without touching Studio arch.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 19 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.124] — 2026-07-09 — `fe172cee`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+21/-1)

**Message:**
> feat(sale): re-enable Project budget/inventory gate on Confirm
>
> Restores the Project-only workflow gates that were dropped from
> the Confirm invisible expression. Bundles five Project-scoped
> predicates under a single quotation_type == 'Project' guard:
>   1. Budget must exist (x_studio_budget_created)
>   2. Budget status must be advanced beyond draft/cancel/confirm
>      — must be 'validate' or 'done'
>   3. No inventory shortages / sub-contract lines pending
>      (x_studio_inventory_short)
>   4. All line prices confirmed by manager
>      (x_studio_price_not_confirmed)
>   5. No un-approved new-item lines
>      (x_studio_new_item_from_project)
> Every predicate's compute already carries an internal
> `quotation_type == 'Project'` short-circuit, so the outer type
> guard is defensive (makes intent explicit + saves the compute
> runtime cost on Sales/Repair quotations).
> Real usage:
>   - proj_budget_status: 4 records at 'confirm', 2 at 'draft'
>   - budget_created: 6 records True
>   - Others: 0 tripped currently (compute-derived, so tripping
>     depends on line-level state)
> Final batch — completes the Confirm gate restore. Full expression
> is now:
>   Universal:  state, credit-limit, bank-guarantee, overdue,
>               valid-order-lines, expired
>   Repair:     RUG cycle, customer-signature, stock
>   Non-Repair: over-commission, margin
>   Project:    budget flow (5 predicates)

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 22 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+9/-1)

**Message:**
> feat(sale): re-enable Bank-Guarantee gate on Confirm (Credit only)
>
> Parallels the credit-limit gate: customers who require a bank
> guarantee have a BG ceiling, and if the SO amount + open credit
> exceeds it, Confirm must wait for BG approval. Only applies to
> Credit-payment SOs (cash customers don't have BG mandates).
> Predicate:
>     x_studio_order_payment_method == 'Credit'
>     and x_studio_over_bank_guarantee
>     and not x_studio_bank_guarantee_approved
> Real usage: x_studio_over_bank_guarantee currently reads False
> on every record (0/886 tripped), meaning either no customer has
> exceeded their BG amount yet OR the compute needs its dependent
> partner data to be up-to-date. Adding the gate now ensures the
> predicate fires the moment a customer does trip the limit — the
> approval workflow is present in the arch (Request Bank Guarantee
> Approval + Approve Bank Guarantee buttons exist).

## [17.0.1.0.118] — 2026-07-09 — `77c86977`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+7/-1)

**Message:**
> feat(sale): re-enable Overdue-debt gate on Confirm (universal)
>
> Restores one of the Studio predicates that was silently dropped
> when Fix-repair's _get_view started rewriting the action_confirm
> invisible expression. Applied universally (no quotation_type
> guard) — an overdue customer should be blocked from confirming
> new orders of any type until an override approval is granted.
> Predicate:
>     x_studio_overdue and not x_studio_overdue_approved
> Real usage on staging before this restore: 40 records tripped,
> 11 approved — so the workflow was live in the business but had
> been going unenforced. Both fields declared in the _get_view
> helper-fields injector so view validation passes.
> First of a batch — adding the previously-dropped Studio approval
> gates back one at a time in priority order. Next: valid order
> lines (universal).

## [17.0.1.0.117] — 2026-07-09 — `e3456164`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_move.py (+8/-4)

**Message:**
> fix(invoice): treat customer-pays repairs as non-RUG invoices
>
> is_rug_invoice compute previously returned True for any invoice
> whose SO had quotation_type == 'Repair' (and no rug-rejection).
> After v115 unified all repairs under quotation_type='Repair', that
> included customer-pays repairs (formerly quotation_type == 'Not
> Under Warranty'). Consequences:
>   • "Change to RUG Account" button appeared on customer-pays
>     invoices where there's no RUG account to change to
>   • action_post's _rug_auto_settle() ran on customer-pays invoices,
>     auto-settling them via the RUG clearing account when they
>     should behave like normal customer invoices (Register Payment,
>     real bank movement)
> Fix: gate is_rug_invoice on `not so.x_repair_customer_pays` so
> warranty repairs only. Both the button visibility and the auto-
> settle guard read the same compute, so this single change fixes
> both symptoms.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 9 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

> the RUG cycle.

## [17.0.1.0.115] — 2026-07-09 — `2a7a476b`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field** (4):
  - account_payment_register.py (+1/-1)
  - project_task.py (+31/-13)
  - sale_order.py (+124/-51)
  - stock_picking.py (+9/-7)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+8/-3)

**Message:**
> refactor(sale): eliminate 'Not Under Warranty' quotation type
>
> Previously the customer-pays semantic was encoded as a distinct
> value of the Studio x_studio_quotation_type selection field
> ('Not Under Warranty' vs 'Repair'), coupling a display-type
> value to a business flag. Every repair SO is now
> quotation_type='Repair'; the customer-pays semantic is captured
> independently by a new Boolean field on sale.order.
> Model changes
>   sale.order
>     + x_repair_customer_pays  Boolean  label 'Not Under Warranty'
>       True when this repair is not covered by warranty (customer
>       pays from the start). Orthogonal to x_studio_rug_rejected
>       (warranty repair that fell through to customer-pays).
> Migration (runs on install/upgrade, idempotent)
>   _migrate_nuw_to_customer_pays_flag
>     1. Rewrites every sale.order with
>        quotation_type == 'Not Under Warranty' to
>        quotation_type = 'Repair' + x_repair_customer_pays = True.
>     2. Deletes the 'Not Under Warranty' ir.model.fields.selection
>        row so the value disappears from the dropdown.
>   Replaces _ensure_not_under_warranty_selection (removed).
> Auto-sync at SO creation
>   project_task._sync_repair_flags (renamed from _sync_quotation_type)
>     Runs from _fsm_ensure_sale_order immediately after
>     _fsm_create_sale_order — so the SO carries the correct
>     quotation_type + x_repair_customer_pays in the same
>     transaction as its creation. Also runs on task write() when
>     sale_order_id is (re)linked. Both fields are written in one
>     write() so downstream subscribers see a consistent record.
> Downstream call sites updated (`quotation_type == 'Not Under
> Warranty'` → `x_repair_customer_pays`)
>   sale_order._get_view          Confirm button gate, Send by Email
>                                 button gate, helper-fields injector
>   sale_order.action_quotation_send
>   sale_order.action_confirm     portal-skip filter + post-confirm
>                                 stage move
>   stock_picking._compute_nuw_block_validate
>   stock_picking._action_done    Path C (customer-pays pickings)
>   account_payment_register.action_create_payments
> Confirm button expression (new)
>   (state not in ('draft', 'sent'))
>    or (x_studio_order_payment_method == 'Credit'
>        and x_studio_over_credit
>        and not x_studio_credit_limit_approved)
>    or (x_studio_quotation_type == 'Repair' and (
>         (not x_repair_customer_pays and not x_studio_rug_approved
>          and not x_studio_rug_rejected)
>         or ((x_repair_customer_pays or x_studio_rug_rejected)
>             and not x_customer_signed)
>         or (not x_repair_stock_ok)
>       ))
> Customer-signature gate reintroduced under the Repair umbrella:
> customer-pays repairs (formerly NUW type) now require the
> customer to sign the portal quote before backend Confirm — same
> behaviour as before, just expressed via the new flag rather than
> the old type value.
> Data preserved: existing NUW records get their flag set + type
> rewritten to 'Repair'. No records deleted. Reject-RUG SOs are NOT
> migrated (per the developer's decision to preserve the
> "started-NUW" vs "warranty-rejected" distinction); downstream
> checks retain the `x_repair_customer_pays or x_studio_rug_rejected`
> disjunct.

**Stats:** 6 files changed, 174 insertions(+), 76 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.114] — 2026-07-09 — `9216f7e2`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+29/-13)

**Message:**
> refactor(sale): scope repair-workflow Confirm gates to Repair type only
>
> Previously the invisible expression on the secondary action_confirm
> button mixed universal predicates (state, credit limit) with
> repair-workflow predicates (RUG approval, customer signature,
> x_repair_stock_ok) as flat OR branches. Non-Repair quotation types
> were incidentally shielded either because the predicate itself
> carried a type check (Repair-scoped clauses) or because
> x_repair_stock_ok computes to True for non-repair SOs — but the
> NUW customer-signature clause fired for any SO with
> x_studio_quotation_type == 'Not Under Warranty', and the flat
> structure made the intent hard to read.
> Refactor into two clearly-labelled groups:
>   Universal (all types):
>     - state check (draft/sent)
>     - credit-limit gate on Credit-payment SOs
>   Repair-only (x_studio_quotation_type == 'Repair'):
>     - RUG cycle resolved
>     - Reject-RUG customer-signature
>     - x_repair_stock_ok
> Sales / Project / Not Under Warranty now pass through ONLY the
> universal gates. NUW's prior customer-signature gate is
> intentionally dropped per the developer's decision to treat only
> quotation_type == 'Repair' as a repair sales order for gating
> purposes. If NUW SOs should still require signature before confirm,
> re-add the clause under a top-level OR branch scoped to
> `x_studio_quotation_type == 'Not Under Warranty'`.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 30 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.111] — 2026-07-07 — `1a9157b7`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+63/-23)

**Message:**
> fix: also seed payment_account_id (Outstanding Receipts) on Advance Payment method lines
>
> v110 added the Manual method line but Odoo 17 also requires a
> payment_account_id — an 'Outstanding Receipts' style asset_current
> account — to be resolvable from one of:
>   1. the method line's payment_account_id (narrowest, wins if set)
>   2. res.company.account_journal_payment_debit_account_id
>   3. res.company.account_journal_payment_credit_account_id
> None were set on the JAM company after v110, so the Create Advance
> Payment click failed with 'You can't create a new payment without an
> outstanding payments/receipts account set...'
> Extend _seed_advance_payment_method_lines to also populate
> payment_account_id on each Manual line:
>   - Look up the per-company 'Outstanding Receipts' asset_current
>     reconcile=True account by name (case-insensitive) and set it as
>     payment_account_id.
>   - Backfill payment_account_id on existing method lines that are
>     missing one (idempotent).
>   - Skip silently when the Outstanding Receipts account cannot be
>     located — better than failing the module upgrade.
> Narrower than setting company-wide defaults (which would affect every
> journal in the company).
> Companion to v110 (_seed_advance_payment_method_lines) and v109
> (button visibility gate) — together the three make the Create
> Advance Payment button a working flow on Sales-type SOs post-confirm.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 64 insertions(+), 24 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.110] — 2026-07-07 — `d95c8937`

**Changed:**

- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+42/-0)

**Message:**
> fix: seed inbound Manual method line on Advance Payment journals
>
> The Studio 'Create Advance Payment' server action creates payments on
> the 'Advance Payment' and 'Advance Payment - Repairs' journals. Odoo
> 17's account.payment validation requires payment_method_line_id to be

> inbound_payment_method_line_ids, so the payment create raised
> 'Please define a payment method line on your payment.'
> Add _seed_advance_payment_method_lines on sale.order that, on every
> install/upgrade, ensures each of those two journals has at least one
> inbound Manual method line. Idempotent: skips journals that already
> have one. Uses account.account_payment_method_manual_in xmlid with a
> code='manual' fallback.
> The Studio journal-type is left as-is (still type='general') — the
> UI-side constraint doesn't block ORM creation of the method line, and
> existing accounting entries on those journals stay intact.
> Companion to _fix_advance_payment_project_field which already patched
> the Studio action to include payment_method_line_id in the create()
> dict.

## [17.0.1.0.108] — 2026-07-02 — `beebb0a2`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+62/-52)

**Message:**
> fix: check availability at factory's resupply pool for Factory Repair
>
> The Factory Repair Location itself typically doesn't stock parts — it
> pulls from central supply warehouses via the warehouse-level
> resupply_wh_ids setting. On staging, RP-JM (the Factory Repair
> Location's warehouse for company 2) has resupply_wh_ids = [BR-EK,
> JM-EK], meaning parts are stocked at those warehouses and pulled into
> RP-JM on demand. v107 was checking free_qty at RP-JM/Stock itself,
> which is always ~0.
> New behaviour for Factory Repair:
>   1. Resolve the Factory Repair Location (per-company config as before).
>   2. Read its warehouse.resupply_wh_ids and use the lot_stock_id of
>      each as the parts pool. Availability = sum of free_qty across the
>      pool.
>   3. Fall back to the factory location itself when resupply_wh_ids is
>      empty, so misconfiguration doesn't silently break the check.
> Centre Repair is unchanged — parts still checked at the ticket's
> x_studio_repair_location (the branch's own stock).
> Refactoring:
>   • _get_repair_stock_source() -> _get_repair_stock_source_locations()
>     now returning a recordset (possibly multi-location for pool sums).
>   • Dropped x_repair_stock_source_id and x_repair_stock_shortage_text
>     computed fields — with a multi-location pool the display fields
>     weren't clean. The UserError modal now names all pool locations
>     inline. Only x_repair_stock_ok remains, which is the only field
>     the view expressions actually reference.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 63 insertions(+), 53 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.107] — 2026-07-02 — `783a524b`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+135/-4)

**Message:**
> feat: gate SO forward buttons on repair-source stock availability
>
> Salespeople should be free to add products to a repair task even when
> the parts aren't in stock — building the quotation is the priority.
> But once the quotation exists, it shouldn't be sent to the customer or
> confirmed until the parts are actually available at the location that
> will consume them:
>   • Factory Repair ticket -> per-company Factory Repair Location
>     (ir.config_parameter 'fix_repair.factory_repair_location.<cid>')
>   • Centre Repair ticket  -> ticket.x_studio_repair_location
>     (the branch's own stock)
> Implementation:
> - New helper `_get_repair_stock_source()` on sale.order walks
>   order.task_id -> helpdesk_ticket_id and resolves the source based on
>   x_studio_job_location. Returns empty recordset for non-repair SOs and
>   for Factory Repair when the company hasn't configured a location.
> - Three non-stored computed fields drive the UI:
>   * x_repair_stock_source_id (m2o stock.location)
>   * x_repair_stock_ok (bool)  — True when every storable line is
>     covered by free_qty at the source, or when there's no source to
>     check (default-unblocked, so non-repair SOs behave as today).
>   * x_repair_stock_shortage_text (char)  — display string for the
>     warning button.
> - action_show_stock_shortage() raises a UserError listing each short
>   line with need/available/shortfall so the salesperson knows exactly
>   what to procure.
> - _get_view:
>   * Injects x_repair_stock_ok as an invisible field so button
>     expressions can reference it.
>   * Adds a btn-danger 'Not Enough Stock' button visible only when
>     x_repair_stock_ok is False and state in ('draft','sent').
>   * Appends `or not x_repair_stock_ok` to the invisible expressions
>     of the three forward-progress buttons: action_quotation_send
>     (Send by Email), Studio button 1980 (Request RUG Approval), and
>     the second action_confirm (Confirm).
> Default unblocked when the repair source can't be resolved (e.g.
> Factory Repair on a company without a configured Factory Repair
> Location) so this fix never silently breaks a config gap.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 136 insertions(+), 5 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.106] — 2026-07-02 — `54edf05a`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: stock_return_picking.py (+34/-5)

**Message:**
> fix: scope phantom picking type to the RRL warehouse
>
> The phantom's picking_type_id was being picked as the first outgoing
> type in the company (typically PW-JM's Delivery Orders), so a BR-NE
> ticket produced a phantom with Operation Type 'Production Warehouse -
> (PW-JM): Delivery Orders' and — because super()._create_returns()
> derives the RET's type from the phantom's return_picking_type_id — a
> BR-NE/RET/XXXXX picking whose Operation Type read 'Production Warehouse
> - (PW-JM): Returns'. Mismatch between the <WH> prefix in the name and
> the Operation Type shown on the form.
> Fix: search the outgoing picking type inside the ticket's Return
> Receipt Location warehouse (with a sequence_code='OUT' filter to skip
> PoS Orders and similar). Fall back to any outgoing type in the company
> only when the RRL warehouse has none — preserving the previous
> behavior as a safety net.
> Effect on the derived RET picking:
>   - Branch warehouses (BR-*) don't have a dedicated Returns type;
>     their outgoing type's return_picking_type_id points back to their
>     own Receipts. So BR-NE/RET/XXXXX will now read 'Branch warehouse -
>     Nuwara-Eliya: Receipts' as Operation Type.
>   - PW-JM/RP-JM have dedicated Returns types, so those are unchanged.
> Uses target_warehouse for both the picking type and the PHAN/RET
> sequence rename so name and Operation Type stay in sync.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 35 insertions(+), 6 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.104] — 2026-07-02 — `a19004eb`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: stock_return_picking.py (+45/-5)

**Message:**
> fix: reuse existing phantom in stock.return.picking default_get
>
> Odoo's wizard framework calls default_get every time a stock.return.picking
> wizard is instantiated. The old implementation unconditionally created a
> phantom outgoing picking on each call, so any user action that re-opened
> the wizard (close without submitting, form re-render) produced a fresh
> phantom while the previous one was left as a done, inventory→customer
> orphan. ~217 such orphans had accumulated by the time this fix was
> authored.
> Fix: stamp the phantom's `origin` field with the marker
> 'fix_repair:return-phantom:ticket:<TID>:lot:<LID>' and, on each default_get,
> first search for an existing phantom carrying this marker with no
> downstream reverse yet. Reuse if found; only build fresh when the marker
> turns up nothing (or turns up a phantom that's already been reversed).
> The phantom stays unstamped on x_studio_helpdesk_ticket_id (which would
> surface it in the SO's Delivery smart button) — the `origin` marker is
> enough for our internal lookup and stays invisible to end users.
> Not tackled here: cleanup of the 217 existing orphans; that can be a
> one-off migration once we confirm none of them is referenced downstream.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 46 insertions(+), 6 deletions(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.103] — 2026-07-01 — `3cad85f1`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: stock_picking.py (+274/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+9/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: delegate heavy stock.picking Studio computes to native Python
>
> Eight unstored Studio computes on stock.picking are referenced by the
> picking form (Studio view 2387) and re-evaluated via safe_eval on every
> render — including the render that fires right after button_validate.
> Each rewrite converts the compute string to a one-line delegating call;
> the actual work runs in native Python at CPython speed. Field names and
> values are unchanged.
> Fields delegated:
>   x_studio_cash_full_payment_made, x_studio_fsm_task_done,
>   x_studio_fully_paid_so, x_studio_need_approval,
>   x_studio_repair_payment_made, x_studio_user_location_validation
>   (writes _validation_2 too), x_studio_valid_factory_repair
>   (writes _factory_repair, _received_at_centre, _picking_count too),
>   x_studio_valid_transfer_lines.
> Idempotent: skips any compute whose code already contains the shared
> '# fix_repair:idempotent-v1' marker, or whose code no longer matches
> the per-field guard substring (i.e. manual Studio edit — leave alone).

**Stats:** 3 files changed, 284 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.102] — 2026-07-01 — `0a61a5b7`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+41/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+9/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: restrict PROJ - Notify Transfer Completion to Project transfers on done
>
> Studio automation 199 (PROJ - Notify Transfer Completion) was
> firing on every write of every stock.picking — no filter_domain,
> no filter_pre_domain, no trigger_field_ids. Each fire creates a
> mail.activity ('Project Transfer Completed' To-Do for Janitha).
> Live evidence on the staging DB:
>   - mail.activity for stock.picking: 106,602 rows accumulated
>   - Picking 16861 alone had 4 duplicate activities created within
>     the same second on a single button_validate press
>   - Each button_validate cascade fires 4-5 stock.picking writes
>     (state transitions + date_done + backorder_id + related field
>     writes), each spawning a fresh activity
> That's the direct cause of the 'Validate takes forever on the 3rd
> delivery' slowness — most of the time is spent creating dozens of
> mail.activity records, running the followers/notification cascade
> for each, and updating the activity_ids one2many on the picking.
> Fix — narrow the automation's fire condition to match its declared
> intent (PROJ = Project transfers):
>   filter_pre_domain = [('state', '!=', 'done')]
>   filter_domain     = [('sale_id.x_studio_quotation_type', '=', 'Project'),
>                        ('state', '=', 'done')]
> The activity is still scheduled when a genuine Project SO's picking
> completes. Repair and Not Under Warranty deliveries no longer touch
> it. Non-SO transfers (internal moves, returns) don't touch it either.
> Idempotent: patch only applied when filter_domain and
> filter_pre_domain are both empty (their original state). Any manual
> edit in Studio preserves the user's version on subsequent upgrades.

**Stats:** 3 files changed, 51 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.101] — 2026-07-01 — `b5ec898c`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: account_move.py (+38/-0), sale_order.py (+119/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+13/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: delegate heavy Studio computes to native Python methods
>
> Studio computed fields store their body as a text 'compute' string on
> ir.model.fields, and safe_eval it on every recompute. That parse+eval
> per fire is 10-50× slower than executing the same logic natively.
> For each heavy Studio compute:
>   1. Add a native Python implementation on our own model class
>      (_fix_repair_compute_<name>()) that does the same work at CPython
>      speed.
>   2. Rewrite the Studio compute string to a single delegating call
>      `self._fix_repair_compute_<name>()`. safe_eval now runs one line;
>      all the work happens in native Python.
> Field names, dependencies, stored/related config, and returned values
> all unchanged. Studio UI still recognises the fields; any view or
> filter that reads them gets the same value.
> Fields delegated on sale.order:
>   - x_studio_over_commission        (iterates order_line)
>   - x_studio_over_credit            (partner_invoice_id.credit etc.)
>   - x_studio_over_credit_amount     (partner arithmetic)
>   - x_studio_over_bank_guarantee    (partner + expiry checks)
>   - x_studio_over_bank_guarantee_amount
>   - x_studio_guarantee_status       (partner + expiry)
>   - x_studio_overdue                (partner_invoice_id.total_overdue)
> Field delegated on account.move.line:
>   - x_studio_credit_limit_2  (partner_id.credit_limit)
> Registered in data/fix_repair_data.xml so both delegators run on every
> module upgrade. Idempotent via the shared '# fix_repair:idempotent-v1'
> marker: fields whose compute already contains the marker are left
> alone (protects manual Studio edits done after our rewrite).

**Stats:** 4 files changed, 171 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.100] — 2026-07-01 — `6d535c87`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+46/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+7/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: convert redundant Studio computes to related fields
>
> Two stored Studio fields on sale.order literally recompute
> record.amount_total via safe_eval'd Python:
>   - x_studio_current_tot_amount
>   - x_studio_current_tot_amount_1  (an identical duplicate)
> Both recompute every time amount_total changes — and during invoice
> creation, amount_total is recomputed multiple times as invoice_status
> transitions. Each recompute loads the compute string from ir.model.fields,
> runs safe_eval, and writes the result. On top of a duplicate. And each
> write cascades through all the sale.order write automations.
> New sale.order._optimize_slow_studio_computes converts these into
> related='amount_total' fields (non-stored). Odoo resolves the value
> via native ORM traversal on demand — no safe_eval, no compute cycle,
> no store write. Same value returned to any view/expression that reads
> either field.
> Idempotent: only rewrites fields whose current compute matches the
> redundant pattern. If someone edits the compute in Studio, the field
> is left alone.

**Stats:** 3 files changed, 54 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.99] — 2026-07-01 — `c67476bf`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+41/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: guard SRM Auto Populate (account.move.line) + Update SO (account.move)
>
> Two more Studio actions rewritten by _optimize_slow_write_automations,
> both hitting the invoice-creation path:
> 1. 'SRM - Auto Populate Report Type in Account Move Lines' (act 1732)
>    - Fires on every account.move.line write, no trigger_field filter
>    - Was: search x_sales_report_type unconditionally on every income
>      line, write regardless of current value
>    - Now: skip search entirely when x_studio_sales_report_type already
>      set; only write if actually changing
>    - Impact: 10-15 AML writes per invoice creation × 1 search each ->
>      ~zero searches on second-run invoices; first-run invoices still
>      do the search but skip the redundant writes
> 2. 'SRM - Update Sales Order - Customer Invoice' (act 1733)
>    - Fires once per account.move create
>    - Was: unconditional sale.order search + write of x_studio_sale_id
>    - Now: search only if x_studio_sale_id not yet set; write only if
>      actually different
> Same functional outcome — target values still reached, same code
> paths, same downstream effects. Just no redundant writes.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 42 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

## [17.0.1.0.98] — 2026-07-01 — `d457e57a`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+109/-0)
- **Data records (XML)**: fix_repair_data.xml (+9/-0)

**Message:**
> perf: idempotence guards on four Studio sale.order write automations
>
> The 'Create Draft Invoice' path was slow because four Studio server
> actions fire on every write to sale.order and unconditionally call
> record.write(...):
>   - RR - Track Lock Status (action 2250)
>   - RR - Track Lock Status - 2 (action 2251)
>   - Update Analytic Tag Parameters - Sales Order - User (action 2415)
>   - Project Sales Order Seq.No - 3 (action 2575)
> During invoice creation Odoo writes to the SO 3-5 times (invoice_ids,
> invoice_status, amount_invoiced, etc.). Each write fires all four
> automations. Automation 2575 writes 'name' back to the SO on every
> fire — a self-cascade that doubles every original write and re-fires
> all four automations again. Even Odoo's recursion guard doesn't
> prevent the searches and per-line lookups inside 202/203/235 from
> running each cycle.
> New sale.order._optimize_slow_write_automations rewrites each
> action's code with a guard: compute the target values, compare to
> current, and only call record.write when at least one value would
> change. Same functional outcome (fields end up at the same values),
> but no redundant writes → no cascade.
> Idempotent via an embedded '# fix_repair:idempotent-v1' marker: if
> the action's code already contains it (previous upgrade or a manual
> edit that preserves it), the rewrite is skipped. Manual Studio edits
> are preserved.
> Registered as a <function> call in data/fix_repair_data.xml so it
> runs on every module upgrade.

**Stats:** 3 files changed, 119 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)

---

## [17.0.1.0.96] — 2026-06-30 — `a02f9beb`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: sale_order.py (+14/-9)

**Message:**
> fix: Re-estimate keeps existing pickings; new lines merge into them
>
> Previous version called _action_cancel which cascades into
> picking.action_cancel on every picking attached to the procurement
> group — the existing deliveries got cancelled and new lines created
> fresh pickings on the next Confirm.
> User wants existing pickings preserved; new lines added during
> re-estimate should land in those same pickings.
> Switch to a direct write({'state': 'draft'}) — no cancel cascade,
> procurement_group_id stays intact. After the salesperson edits and
> re-confirms the SO, sale.order.line._action_launch_stock_rule skips
> lines already at qty (no double procurement) and only the new lines
> trigger fresh moves. Those moves merge into the existing pickings via
> the preserved procurement group (standard Odoo merge logic on
> matching picking_type + partner + group).
> Trade-off: the SO's state lineage now has an unconventional direct
> write rather than going through action_cancel + action_draft. The
> form sees state='draft' as expected; signed/signature/RUG fields
> are cleared in the next write.

**Stats:** 2 files changed, 15 insertions(+), 10 deletions(-)

## [17.0.1.0.89] — 2026-06-25 — `afd71c63`

**Changed:**
- **Metadata**: __manifest__.py (version bump) (+1/-1)
- **Model/field**: helpdesk_ticket.py (+22/-2)

**Message:**
> fix: keep repair-flow pickings off the SO Delivery smart button
>
> industry_fsm_stock hooks into stock.move.create — when a task is
> active in env.context (e.g. inside action_fsm_validate after
> Mark as Done), it auto-binds the new move to the task's SO
> procurement group. That makes the Mark as Done picking show up on
> the SO's Delivery smart button, which violates our 'repair pickings
> live on the ticket, not on the SO' design.
> Three layers of defence in _create_repair_transfer:
>   1. Strip default_group_id from the env before create
>   2. Pass group_id=False explicitly in both picking and move create
>   3. Defensive re-write group_id=False on both records before
>      state='done', in case any cascade between create and now set it
>      via another path
> Result: picking.sale_id stays False (it's computed from group_id
> and move.sale_line_id, both of which are False), so the SO Delivery
> smart button never lists these pickings. They remain accessible only
> via the ticket's Movements smart button.

**Message:**
> feat: RUG invoice auto-settles on Confirm — skip Register Payment
>
> When a posted out_invoice is is_rug_invoice and has an unreconciled
> receivable line, action_post now creates an internal clearing entry
> (DR <company RUG account> / CR Debtors) for the receivable balance,
> posts it, and reconciles:
>   - invoice Debtors DR ↔ clearing Debtors CR → invoice payment_state
>     moves straight to 'paid'
>   - invoice RUG CR ↔ clearing RUG DR → both lines net to zero so no
>     real bank movement happens
> No-op if no RUG account is configured for the company, if the invoice
> doesn't have any line on the RUG account ('Change to RUG Account'
> wasn't clicked), or if the receivable is already reconciled. Falls
> back to UserError when no type='general' journal exists for the
> company.
